    By group¶
    

      

    • Motion: the motion commands and their answers
      • 

    • Gains and limits: the loop gains, the limits, Kt
      • 

    • Status requests: temperature, voltage, device info, errors, current, encoder, Kt, identity,
  telemetry, the heartbeat
      • 

    • System: node ID, E-stop, idle, save, reset, clear, watchdog, heartbeat setup, settings
      • 

    • Parameters and buffers: the parameters behind Send_Parameter and Send_Get_Parameter, every one
  of them, and the buffers: capture, histogram, anti-cogging, encoder table
      • 

    • Sound: tones, the queue and samples through the motor
      • 

    • Gripper: the gripper command, calibration and the gripper state
      • 

    

    The Python library¶
    

    Every message the host can send has a method of the same name in Spectral_BLDC.SpectralCAN, listed in
Python library.
    

    Tools and downloads¶
    

    Build or decode a CAN frame, and download this firmware's JSON and DBC definitions.
    Motion¶
    

    The commands that move the motor and the frames that come back.
    

    Send_data_pack_1¶
    

    

    ID 2host → driver2 or 5 or 8 bytesPython: Send_data_pack_1()
    

    

    The motion command. Its length decides the control mode the driver enters: 8 bytes command a position with a speed and a current feedforward, 5 bytes a speed with a current feedforward, 2 bytes a current. Send 0 for a feedforward you do not use. A frame of any other length is ignored (the driver flags it as a wrong data length) but still answered.
    

    8 bytes — position setpoint with speed and current feedforward (position mode)
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0-2
Position setpoint
signed 24-bit
encoder ticks

    

    
3-5
Speed feedforward
signed 24-bit
encoder ticks/s

    

    
6-7
Current feedforward
signed 16-bit
mA

    
    

    

    

    5 bytes — speed setpoint with current feedforward (velocity mode)
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0-2
Speed setpoint
signed 24-bit
encoder ticks/s

    

    
3-4
Current feedforward
signed 16-bit
mA

    
    

    

    

    2 bytes — current setpoint (current mode)
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0-1
Current setpoint
signed 16-bit
mA

    
    

    

    

    The driver answers with Respond_data_pack_1.
    

    Every form resets the watchdog. With Rstint set, the integral accumulators are cleared on each new setpoint. From firmware 109 a position or speed frame that follows another mode (idle, current, PD, a watchdog trip) starts the velocity integral from zero, so a release from a wound-up mode does not kick; one that follows position or velocity mode keeps it. The new setpoint, the mode change and the driver's wake-up reach the control loop together, so the first frame after Idle is followed.
    

    

    Send_data_pack_PD¶
    

    

    ID 4host → driver8 bytesPython: Send_data_pack_PD()
    

    

    Puts the driver in impedance (PD) mode and sets the position, speed and current feedforward of the PD loop, whose gains are KP and KD (Send_PD_Gains).
    

    8 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0-2
Position setpoint
signed 24-bit
encoder ticks

    

    
3-5
Speed setpoint
signed 24-bit
encoder ticks/s

    

    
6-7
Current feedforward
signed 16-bit
mA

    
    

    

    

    The driver answers with Respond_data_pack_1.
    

    Firmware 106 and older wrote the speed into the feedforward instead of the PD loop's speed setpoint, so the speed term had no effect; 107 fixed it.
    

    

    Respond_data_pack_1¶
    

    

    ID 3driver → host8 bytes
    

    

    The driver's answer to Send_data_pack_1 and Send_data_pack_PD: where the motor is, how fast it turns and the current it draws. The Python library stores them as position, speed and current.

encoder ticks

signed 24-bit
encoder ticks/s

    

    
6-7
current
signed 16-bit
Iq in mA

    Send_Iq¶
    

    

    ID 21host → drivernot implemented
    

    

    Reserved; the firmware has no handler for this ID. Use the 2-byte form of Send_data_pack_1 for a current setpoint.
    

    Gains and limits¶
    

    The commands that set the loop gains and the limits. None of them is answered; save with Send_Save_config.

    Send_Limits¶
    

    

    ID 20host → driver8 bytesPython: Send_Limits()

From firmware 109 a negative value, or one that is not a number, is ignored, and the current limit stops at the driver's 2800 mA, as with #Ilim. Firmware 107 and older stored the current limit as it arrived. Never send more than the motor's rating.

    Send_Voltage_Limit¶
    

    

    ID 34host → driver4 bytesPython: Send_Voltage_Limit()
    

    

    Sets the voltage limit of the current loops (the same value as #Vlim), in mV. 0 means use the supply voltage. In the 104 release image (the source at tag V104 does not have it yet) and in every release from 105. From firmware 109 it takes 0 to 60000 mV, as parameter 17 does; a value outside is ignored.

signed 32-bit
mV; 0 = Vbus

    
    

    

    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    Status requests¶
    

    Data the host asks for with a remote frame (RTR bit set, no data); the driver answers with a data frame with the same command ID. And the heartbeat, which the driver sends on its own.
    

    Send_Respond_Ping¶
    

    

    ID 10host → driver as a remote frame, driver → host as a data frameremote frame, 0 bytesPython: Send_Respond_Ping()
    

    

    Asks the driver to answer with an empty frame, to see that it is on the bus.
    

    The frame (0 bytes) carries no data.
    

    The driver answers with Send_Respond_Ping.

    Send_Respond_Identity¶
    

    

    ID 39host → driver as a remote frame, driver → host as a data frameremote frame, 0 bytesPython: Send_Respond_Identity()
    

    

    Asks what the drive is: the product, the tool it is built into, the firmware release, the hardware version, the pole pairs, whether it is calibrated, and eight flags. A host can check it before it commands or flashes a drive. Added in firmware 109. Python attributes product_id, product_name, tool_id, firmware_version, identity_hardware_version, pole_pairs, identity_calibrated, identity_flags.
    

    The frame (8 bytes)
    

    

    


    
Byte
Bit
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0

product
unsigned 8-bit
1 STEPFOC, 2 Spectral Micro: the firmware that answers

    

    
1

tool_id
unsigned 8-bit
parameter 1: 0 none, 1 SSG-48, 11-33 MSG, 200-254 your own

    

    
2-3

firmware_version
unsigned 16-bit
the release, e.g. 109

    

    
4

hardware_version
unsigned 8-bit
PCB revision from the EEPROM

    

    
5

unsigned 8-bit
the motor's pole pairs; 0 if outside 0-255

    

    
6

calibrated
unsigned 8-bit
1 calibrated, 0 not

    

    
7
0
gripper
bit
the driver is set up as a gripper (#Gripper 1)

    

    
7
1
brake_on_idle
bit
idle brakes (1) or coasts (0)

    

    
7

bit
calibration found the coils wired the other way round

the EEPROM has the bank settings block 2 lives in, so parameters 1-5 can be saved

this firmware is on the other product's board (the product lock) and drives nothing

the current-sense zero was measured at start-up and is plausible

    

    
7

7
7

bit
the current-sense zero measured with the bridge switching is in use (parameter 46)

    
    

    

    

    The driver answers with Send_Respond_Identity.
    

    The flags in byte 7 are numbered the usual way, bit 0 the least significant (unlike the error bytes, which the firmware packs from bit 7 down).

    Respond_Heartbeat¶
    

    

    ID 9driver → host0 bytes
    

    

    The frame the driver sends on its own at the heartbeat interval (Send_Heartbeat_Setup); empty. Off by default. Any other frame the driver sends resets the heartbeat timer, so a driver that answers motion commands at a high rate never sends a heartbeat.
    

    The frame (0 bytes) carries no data.
    

    System¶
    

    Identity, saving, resetting, errors, the watchdog, the heartbeat and the settings.
    

    Send_CAN_ID¶
    

    

    ID 11host → driver1 bytesPython: Send_CAN_ID()
    

    

    Gives the driver a new node ID, 0 to 13 (the firmware accepts up to 15, but the bootloader reserves 14 and 15: a driver on one of those cannot be updated over CAN). The driver answers to the new ID at once. The ID is kept over a power cycle only after Send_Save_config, and saving is also what copies it into the bootloader's board ID.
    

    1 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0
ID
unsigned 8-bit
0 to 13 (14 and 15 are accepted but reserved by the bootloader)

    
    

    

    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    

    Send_ESTOP¶
    

    

    ID 0host → driver0 bytesPython: Send_ESTOP()
    

    

    Stops the motor and raises the E-stop error, which stays until Send_Clear_Error.
    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    

    Send_Idle¶
    

    

    ID 12host → driver0 bytesPython: Send_Idle()
    

    

    Puts the driver in idle mode: the motor coasts, or brakes when Send_Settings or #Brake 1 asked for it.
    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    

    Send_Save_config¶
    

    

    ID 13host → driver0 bytesPython: Send_Save_config()
    

    

    Writes the current configuration (gains, limits, calibration, CAN ID, heartbeat, watchdog, gripper flag) to the EEPROM, and copies the CAN ID into the bootloader's board ID. From firmware 109 it also writes the tool id and the watchdog fallback (settings block 2), the anti-cogging set with its mode and scale, and the encoder table with its on/off flag, parameter 56 and the current-sense zero trims, each block with its own checksum; of the two large blocks only the 16-byte pages that changed are written. From firmware 110 it also writes the idle behaviour of Send_Settings (parameter 18). Not kept: the current loop bandwidth, and every parameter the parameter table marks as not kept. A drive stopped by the product lock writes nothing.
    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    

    Send_Reset¶
    

    

    ID 14host → driver0 bytesPython: Send_Reset()
    

    

    Resets the driver. From firmware 104 the reset lands in the bootloader, which waits 2 s for a flashing host and then starts the firmware again; this is how a firmware update begins.
    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    

    Send_Clear_Error¶
    

    

    ID 1host → driver0 bytesPython: Send_Clear_Error()
    

    

    Clears the error flags and goes to idle; it also ends a watchdog hold or descend. An error whose cause is still present comes straight back. A calibration error is cleared only by calibrating.
    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    

    Send_Watchdog¶
    

    

    ID 15host → driver5 bytesPython: Send_Watchdog()
    

    

    Sets the watchdog. The motion commands (Send_data_pack_1, Send_data_pack_PD, Send_gripper_data_pack) and the requests the driver answers (the Send_Respond_ remote frames, Send_Get_Parameter, Send_Buffer_Read) reset it; the other frames (Send_Parameter among them) and UART commands do not. When the time runs out the driver reports a watchdog error and does what the action byte says. 0 turns it off (the default). From firmware 109 the driver acts on the action byte, but only when the watchdog is the only active error: any other error gets the legacy response, the motor disabled and coasting. Hold and descend run for the fallback time (parameter 2, 2 s by default), then the after-action (parameter 5); they start only if the driver was in position, velocity, current, PD or gripper mode, otherwise the driver gives the legacy response and the motor coasts. Setting the time starts a fresh window, and a negative time means off. The watchdog explains each action. The setting is kept by Send_Save_config. The same values are parameters 6 and 7, which #Par reaches over UART: to turn off a saved watchdog, send 0 over CAN or #Par 6,0, and save.
    

    5 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0-3
Time
unsigned 32-bit
ms; 0 = off

    

    
4
Action
unsigned 8-bit
0 legacy (the motor disabled, coasting), 1 hold the position, 2 brake (not current-limited), 3 coast, 4 descend at the fallback current and speed; other values act as 0

    
    

    

    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    Firmware 107 and older store the action byte without acting on it. The Python library sends all five actions from 1.21 ('Idle', 'Hold', 'Brake', 'Coast', 'Descend'); 1.20 and older send only 0, 1 or 2.
    

    

    Send_Heartbeat_Setup¶
    

    

    ID 30host → driver4 bytesPython: Send_Heartbeat_Setup()
    

    

    Sets the heartbeat interval in ms; 0 turns the heartbeat off (the default). Kept by Send_Save_config.
    

    4 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0-3
Interval
unsigned 32-bit
ms; 0 = off

    
    

    

    

    

    Send_Settings¶
    

    

    ID 35host → driver2 bytesPython: Send_Settings()
    

    

    Sets boolean settings that have no command of their own: a mask byte says which settings the frame touches, a value byte gives their new values; settings outside the mask are left alone. Bit 0 is the idle behaviour: 1 brake (phases shorted), 0 coast (the default). From firmware 110 Send_Save_config keeps it; on 109 the driver coasted again after a power cycle, as a driver still does that never saved it. Braking applies only in idle without an error; an error always disables the motor, which then coasts. The braking current is not limited, see Control modes. Added in firmware 107 (STEPFOC 107). The Python library has Send_Settings(mask, value) and, for this bit, Send_Brake_Coast(brake) from 1.21; 1.20 and older have no call for it.
    

    2 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0
Mask
unsigned 8-bit
bit 0 = write the idle behaviour

    

    
1
Value
unsigned 8-bit
bit 0: 1 brake, 0 coast

    
    

    

    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    

    Send_Cyclic_command¶
    

    

    ID 29host → drivernot implemented
    

    

    Reserved. The firmware has no handler for this ID and the Python library's call does nothing.
    

    Parameters and buffers¶
    

    From firmware 109 a setting that has no command of its own is a parameter: a number from 1 to 93 (89 in firmware
109) behind one pair of commands, Send_Parameter (ID 36) to set it and Send_Get_Parameter (ID 37) to read it,
answered with Respond_Parameter (ID 38). The identifier has room for only 64 command IDs, so new settings no longer
use them up.
    

    Parameter numbers are permanent: a number is never given a new meaning. Number 73 is not used and stays free.
Parameters 84 to 87 are read only and say which build and which board answer: the git commit the firmware was built
from (84), its build time in Unix seconds (85), the board ID (86) and the calibration ID (87). #Info prints them
as Build, Board ID and Calibration ID (Status and diagnostics).
Parameters 88 and 89, a diagnostic of when the current sensors are read, are the Spectral Micro's own; STEPFOC does
not have them.
    

    Firmware 110 adds 90 to 93: the three settings that only UART could set before, #Rstint (90), #Term, the
thermistor (91) and #Gripper, gripper mode (92), and the gripper's stroke (93, read only). The Spectral-BLDC
library 1.22 names them (PARAM_RSTINT, PARAM_THERMISTOR, PARAM_GRIPPER_MODE, PARAM_GRIPPER_STROKE); 1.21
sets and reads them by their numbers.
    

    The buffers are the larger data a drive holds: a capture of three signals, a histogram, the anti-cogging set
and the encoder table. Send_Buffer_Read (ID 41) reads them in 8-byte frames, Respond_Buffer_Data (ID 42) carries
them, and Send_Buffer_Write (ID 43) writes the anti-cogging set.
    

    Over UART, #Par does the same: #Par lists every parameter, #Par 1 reads one, #Par 1,200 sets it
(Status and utility).
    

    Setting and reading a parameter¶
    

    A set is not answered: read the value back to know what the driver took. A value outside a parameter's range is
clamped, replaced or refused, as the table says, and a read-only number ignores the write.
    

    import Spectral_BLDC as Spectral

comms = Spectral.CanCommunication(bustype='slcan', channel='COM41', bitrate=1000000)
motor = Spectral.SpectralCAN(node_id=0, communication=comms)

motor.Send_Parameter(1, 200)               # tool id 200, a custom tool
motor.Send_Get_Parameter(1)                # read it back
message, ids = comms.receive_can_messages(timeout=0.1)
if message is not None:
    motor.UnpackData(message, ids)
print(motor.parameters.get(1))
motor.Send_Save_config()                   # keep it over a power cycle

    

    Send_Parameter, Send_Get_Parameter and the buffer calls need the Spectral-BLDC library 1.21 or newer.
Send_Parameter takes the value itself: a gain or a limit (8 to 16 and 20) is sent as a float, so
motor.Send_Parameter(16, 1500) sets a current limit of 1500 mA; every other parameter takes a whole number.
    

    The commands¶
    

    Send_Parameter¶
    

    

    ID 36host → driver5 bytesPython: Send_Parameter()
    

    

    Sets one parameter by its number (the parameter table). Firmware 109 gives new settings a parameter number instead of a command ID of their own, since the identifier has room for only 64 command IDs. A value outside a parameter's range is clamped, replaced or refused as the table says; a number the firmware does not know, or a read-only one, is ignored. The driver does not answer: read the value back with Send_Get_Parameter. A value set this way lives in RAM until Send_Save_config, like every other setting; the table says which parameters Save keeps.
    

    5 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0
Parameter
unsigned 8-bit
the parameter number, 1-93

    

    
1-4
Value
signed 32-bit
the new value, in the parameter's unit; for the gains, limits and Kt (8-16, 20) a float's bit pattern

    
    

    

    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    A data frame of another length, or a remote frame, is ignored and flagged as a wrong data length. It does not reset the watchdog.
    

    

    Send_Get_Parameter¶
    

    

    ID 37host → driver1 bytesPython: Send_Get_Parameter()
    

    

    Asks for the value in force of one parameter. The answer, Respond_Parameter, comes under ID 38, not 37: a host and a driver must never send data frames with the same identifier, because two sent at the same moment collide. Resets the watchdog. The Python library stores the value in parameters[number].
    

    1 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0
Parameter
unsigned 8-bit
the parameter number

    
    

    

    

    The driver answers with Respond_Parameter.
    

    

    Respond_Parameter¶
    

    

    ID 38driver → host5 bytes
    

    

    The answer to Send_Get_Parameter: the number and the value in force. For a number the firmware does not know the frame carries the number alone (1 byte; the Python library sets unknown_parameter). The gains, limits and Kt (parameters 8 to 16 and 20) travel as the bit pattern of an IEEE-754 float, the others as integers.
    

    The frame (5 bytes)
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0
parameter
unsigned 8-bit
the parameter number

    

    
1-4
value
signed 32-bit
the value in force; a float's bits for 8-16 and 20

    
    

    

    

    

    Send_Buffer_Read¶
    

    

    ID 41host → driver5 bytesPython: Send_Buffer_Read()
    

    

    Asks for entries of one of the drive's buffers: the capture, the histogram, the anti-cogging harmonics or table, or the encoder table. The driver answers with one Respond_Buffer_Data frame per entry (for the two tables, one per three values), sent from its main loop whenever a transmit mailbox is free, so a long read-out never holds up the control loop or other answers. A new request replaces one still in progress; count 0 cancels it. Entries past the end are not sent. Resets the watchdog.
    

    5 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0
Kind
unsigned 8-bit
0 capture (up to 1024 samples: parameter 32 says how many), 1 histogram (256 bins), 2 anti-cogging harmonics (8 slots), 3 anti-cogging table (256 values), 4 encoder table (128 values)

    

    
1-2
First
unsigned 16-bit
the first index

    

    
3-4
Count
unsigned 16-bit
how many entries; 0 cancels a read-out

    
    

    

    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    

    Respond_Buffer_Data¶
    

    

    ID 42driver → host8 bytes
    

    

    One entry of a buffer, the answer to Send_Buffer_Read. The first two bytes say which: the kind in the top three bits, the index in the other thirteen (kind × 8192 + index). The six bytes after them depend on the kind, as the table below the frame says.
    

    The frame (8 bytes)
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0-1
header
unsigned 16-bit
kind × 8192 + index

    

    
2-3
word_a
signed 16-bit
capture: channel A; histogram: the count (unsigned); harmonics: the harmonic number (0 = slot unused); tables: entry index

    

    
4-5
word_b
signed 16-bit
capture: channel B; histogram: the sum's upper 16 bits; harmonics: a, the cosine term in mA; tables: entry index + 1

    

    
6-7
word_c
signed 16-bit
capture: channel C; histogram: the sum's lower 16 bits; harmonics: b, the sine term in mA; tables: entry index + 2 (0 past the end)

    
    

    

    

    


    
Kind
Index
Bytes 2-7

    
    


    
0 capture
the sample, oldest first, 0 to parameter 32 − 1
channels A, B and C, each in its signal's unit (the parameter table lists the signals)

    

    
1 histogram
the bin, 0-255 over one electrical turn or one motor turn
the count (unsigned 16-bit), then the sum (signed 32-bit); the bin's mean is sum ÷ count

    

    
2 anti-cogging harmonics
the slot, 0-7
the harmonic of the electrical angle (1-32, 0 = unused), a and b in mA: the slot adds a·cos(h·θ) + b·sin(h·θ)

    

    
3 anti-cogging table
the first of three values, 0-255
three values in mA, 64 angle counts apart (256 per electrical turn)

    

    
4 encoder table
the first of three values, 0-127
three corrections in 1/16 encoder count, 128 per motor turn

    
    

    

    

    Send_Buffer_Write¶
    

    

    ID 43host → driver8 bytesPython: Send_Cogging_Harmonic(), Send_Cogging_Table()
    

    

    Writes the anti-cogging set: kind 2 one harmonic slot per frame, kind 3 three table values per frame, in the Respond_Buffer_Data layout. Other kinds are ignored (the encoder table cannot be written), and so is a harmonic above 32. The set lives in RAM until Send_Save_config; switch it on with parameter 37. The driver does not answer.
    

    8 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0-1
Header
unsigned 16-bit
kind × 8192 + index; kind 2 or 3

    

    
2-3
Word A
signed 16-bit
kind 2: the harmonic (1-32, 0 = unused); kind 3: value at index

    

    
4-5
Word B
signed 16-bit
kind 2: a in mA; kind 3: value at index + 1

    

    
6-7
Word C
signed 16-bit
kind 2: b in mA; kind 3: value at index + 2

    
    

    

    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    

    Every parameter¶
    

    The number and the name are read from the firmware; the rest is checked against its source. Access: a read-only
parameter ignores a write; a run parameter starts or stops a run when written and gives its state when read.
    

    Watchdog and tool¶
    

    Parameters 1 to 5 live in settings block 2 of the EEPROM, 6 and 7 in the first settings block beside the other settings; Send_Save_config keeps all seven. A value outside a range is clamped, replaced or ignored, as the Value column says.
    

    

    


    
No.
What it is
Value

    
    


    
1
What the drive is built into. For an MSG the tens digit is the rail (1 = 100, 2 = 150, 3 = 200 mm) and the units digit the stepper (1 = 21.5, 2 = 40, 3 = 60 mm). Also #Tool; reported in the identity frame.
    PARAM_TOOL_ID · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, settings block 2    		
0 none, 1 SSG-48, 11-33 MSG, 200-254 your own tools; a value outside 0-255 is ignored. Default 0

    

    
2
How long watchdog hold or descend runs before the after-action takes over.
    PARAM_WD_FALLBACK_TIME · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, settings block 2    		
ms, 0-60000 (clamped). Default 2000

    

    
3
The current watchdog descend applies. 0 lets gravity lower a joint; a gripper gets its grip current.
    PARAM_WD_FALLBACK_CURRENT · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, settings block 2    		
mA, signed, -32768 to 32767 (outside: 0). Default 0

    

    
4
The speed watchdog descend never exceeds, in either direction.
    PARAM_WD_FALLBACK_SPEED · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, settings block 2    		
ticks/s, 0-65535 (outside: 1000). Default 1000

    

    
5
What follows hold or descend when the fallback time has run out.
    PARAM_WD_AFTER_ACTION · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, settings block 2    		
0 legacy, 2 brake, 3 coast (any other value: 0). Default 0

    

    
6
The watchdog time, the same value Send_Watchdog sets.
    PARAM_WD_TIME · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, settings block 1    		
ms, 0 = off (a negative value: 0). Default 0

    

    
7
What a trip of the watchdog alone does, the same value as byte 4 of Send_Watchdog.
    PARAM_WD_ACTION · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, settings block 1    		
0 legacy, 1 hold, 2 brake, 3 coast, 4 descend (outside 0-255: 0; 5-255 act as legacy). Default 0

    
    

    

    

    Gains, limits and settings¶
    

    The values in force, readable and writable by parameter (their own commands, given in each row, still work). A float travels as its IEEE-754 bit pattern, big-endian, the way the gain commands send it; over UART #Par 9,0.02 sets Kpv. A write goes to RAM like the commands' own; Send_Save_config keeps it. Parameter 23, the product stamp, stays read only.
    

    

    


    
No.
What it is
Value

float; 0 or more, below 10^9 (negative or not a number: refused)

    PARAM_GRIPPER_MODE · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes

    Current sensing and the current loop¶
    

    The current-sense zero is the ADC reading with no current flowing, in counts from 2048 (1 count is about 1.6 mA). The driver measures it at every start-up with the bridge off; the trim is added to it.
    

    

    


    
No.
What it is
Value

    
    


    
21
The current-sense zero of channel 1 in use. Measured at start-up (the trim, 59, added); writable for tests, until the next start-up.
    PARAM_SENSE1_OFFSET · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: no: measured again at every start-up    		
counts from 2048, -400 to 400 (outside: refused)

    

    
22
The current-sense zero of channel 2 in use, as 21.
    PARAM_SENSE2_OFFSET · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: no: measured again at every start-up    		
counts from 2048, -400 to 400 (outside: refused)

    

    
40
The zero of channel 1 measured with the bridge off, the trim added.
    PARAM_SENSE1_OFFSET_OFF · Access: read only · Kept by Save: no: measured at every start-up    		
counts from 2048

    

    
41
The zero of channel 2 measured with the bridge off, the trim added.
    PARAM_SENSE2_OFFSET_OFF · Access: read only · Kept by Save: no: measured at every start-up    		
counts from 2048

    

    
42
The second zero measurement, made in idle after start-up with the bridge switching at 50 % duty. On the bench it made the current ripple worse, so it is measured and kept but not used unless 46 asks for it.
    PARAM_SENSE_ZERO_SWITCHING · Access: read only · Kept by Save: no    		
0 not run, 1 measuring, 2 in use, 3 rejected (implausible), 4 interrupted (a command or an error came first), 5 measured, not in use

    PARAM_SENSE_ZERO_USE_SWITCHING · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: no

    PARAM_SENSE2_OFFSET_SW · Access: read only · Kept by Save: no

    PARAM_ID_SETPOINT · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: no    		
mA, -3000 to 3000 (outside: refused). Default 0

    PARAM_ZERO_TRIM1 · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, encoder block

    PARAM_ZERO_TRIM2 · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, encoder block    		
counts, -60 to 60 (outside: refused). Default 0

    PARAM_THETA_OFFSET · Access: read only · Kept by Save: yes, with the calibration

    PARAM_TRIM_H1_BEFORE · Access: read only · Kept by Save: no    		
0.1 mA

    

    
63

    PARAM_TRIM_H1_AFTER · Access: read only · Kept by Save: no    		
0.1 mA

    

    
64
Commutation delay compensation: the output voltage is turned forward by the angle the rotor covers in this time. Applied in the closed-loop modes above 100 ticks/s. 234 µs is the Spectral Micro's own delay, measured on the bench.
    PARAM_COMM_DELAY_US · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: no: 234 at every start-up    		
µs, 0-500 (outside: refused), 0 = off. Default 234

    

    
65
Where the transforms of every control loop take sin and cos from. On the STEPFOC bench the table was not faster.
    PARAM_FAST_TRIG · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: no    		
1 from the integer sine table, 0 from the float routine. Default 0

    

    
88
An experimental diagnostic of when the current sensors are read, off by default. They are read by a software start, wherever the code happens to be in the 25 kHz centre-aligned PWM cycle, so the moment of a reading depends on the code before it. With a count set, the driver waits until the PWM counter is within 24 counts of it (at most about 60 µs), then reads. Leave it at -1 unless you are investigating the current sensing.
    PARAM_SENSE_SAMPLE_AT · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: no: -1 at every start-up    		
-1: read wherever the code is (the default); 0 to the PWM period (the timer's reload value): wait for that count first (outside: refused)

    

    
89
Where in the PWM cycle the last current reading happened; it shows where parameter 88 puts the readings.
    PARAM_SENSE_SAMPLE_SEEN · Access: read only · Kept by Save: —    		
the PWM counter at the last reading, +65536 while it counts down

    
    

    

    

    Speed estimate and the velocity loop¶

What it is
Value

The position input filter, position and PD modes: a critically damped filter between the host's position setpoints and the loops, which also makes the speed from them. Only for hosts that send positions without the speed; about the host's setpoint rate ÷ 12.
    PARAM_INPUT_FILTER_HZ · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: no

    PARAM_VEL_INT_DEADBAND · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: no

    Anti-cogging¶
    

    

    


    
No.
What it is
Value

    
    


    
37
Which form of the stored anti-cogging is added to the current setpoint in velocity and position modes. 1 or 2 is refused while the stored set holds nothing for that form: read it back.
    PARAM_COGGING_MODE · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, anti-cogging block    		
0 off, 1 harmonics, 2 table. Default 0

    

    
38
How much of the stored anti-cogging is applied.
    PARAM_COGGING_SCALE · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, anti-cogging block    		
percent, 0-200 (clamped). Default 100

    

    
43
The anti-cogging identification on the drive: one motor turn forward and one back in velocity mode at the speed of 44. The shaft must be free to turn. A command from a host or an error aborts it. On the Spectral Micro 4 and 5 identify like 1 and 2 (the trim step is skipped); not yet tested on its bench.
    PARAM_COGCAL · Access: set starts or stops a run; read gives its state · Kept by Save: the result, when started with 2, 3 or 5    		
Write 0 abort, 1 identify, 2 identify and save, 3 encoder table, then anti-cogging, then save all, 4 identify with the current-sense zero trim, 5 the same and save. Read 0 idle, 1 turning forward, 2 turning backward, 3 done (harmonics on), 4 aborted, 5 failed (an error, or not calibrated), 6 failed (the shaft did not turn), 7 a combined run is measuring the encoder table, 8 the combined run's encoder table failed (52 says why), 9 not used, 10 switched off (the encoder table was measured again on its own), 11 done without the trim

    

    
44
The speed the identification turns at.
    PARAM_COGCAL_SPEED · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: no    		
ticks/s, 100-20000 (clamped). Default 1500

    

    
45
The cogging current the last identification found, rms.
    PARAM_COGCAL_RIPPLE · Access: read only · Kept by Save: no    		
0.1 mA rms

    
    

    

    

    Encoder table¶
    

    

    


    
No.
What it is
Value

    
    


    
52
Measures the encoder table: the field turns the rotor open loop one shaft turn forward and one back at 2048 ticks/s (about 20 s). The shaft must be free.
    PARAM_ENCAL · Access: set starts or stops a run; read gives its state · Kept by Save: the table, when started with 2 or 3    		
Write 0 abort, 1 measure and use, 2 measure, use and save, 3 then the anti-cogging and save all. Read 0 idle, 1 turning forward, 2 turning backward, 3 done (in use), 4 aborted, 5 failed (an error, or not calibrated), 6 failed (the rotor did not follow the field), 7 rejected (over 4° peak to peak, or an offset move over 60° electrical)

    

    
53
Corrects the encoder reading with the table.
    PARAM_ENCAL_USE · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, encoder block    		
1 on, 0 off; 1 only when a table was measured; refused while one is being measured

    

    
54
The table's error, peak to peak (0 without a table).
    PARAM_ENCAL_PP · Access: read only · Kept by Save: with the table    		
0.01° of the shaft

    

    
55

    PARAM_ENCAL_ONCE · Access: read only · Kept by Save: with the table    		
0.01° of the shaft

No effect on the Spectral Micro: #Cal always runs the encoder table and the anti-cogging, and #Calonly never does. It stays so that the parameter numbers match STEPFOC.
    PARAM_AFTER_CAL · Access: set, read · Kept by Save: yes, encoder block    		
0, 1 or 2 (outside: refused). Default 0

    PARAM_ENCAL_OFFSET · Access: read only · Kept by Save: no    		
0.1° electrical

    
    

    

    

    Capture and histogram¶
    

    The signal numbers: 0 none, 1 measured Iq (mA), 2 measured Id (mA), 3 Iq setpoint with the anti-cogging (mA), 4 speed (ticks/s, saturates at ±32767), 5 speed ÷ 16, 6 position (ticks, the low 16 bits), 7 position error (ticks), 8 electrical angle (0-16383 per electrical turn, from the encoder alone), 9 encoder (0-16383 per motor turn), 10 Vbus (10 mV), 11 and 12 the two current-sense readings (counts after the zero), 13 the control interrupt's time (µs), 14 the anti-cogging feedforward (mA), 15 the Iq setpoint without the anti-cogging (mA), 16 the Uq setpoint (mV), 17 and 18 the d and q voltage the output transform received (mV). None of these settings is saved.
    

    

    


    
No.
What it is
Value

signal 0-18 (outside: 0). Default 4, speed

signal 0-18 (outside: 0). Default 1, Iq

signal 0-18 (outside: 0). Default 8, electrical angle

    What is in force: parameter 79¶
    

    Parameter 79 says in one read what the drive runs with. #Info prints the same at its end, under In force:.
Bit 0 is the least significant.

    Reading the capture¶
    

    A capture records three signals inside the control loop, one sample every divider loops, 1024 samples per
channel. It starts when parameter 28 is written: at once (1), when the error flag rises (2, a watchdog trip
included), or when channel A rises (3) or falls (4) through the level of parameter 29. Parameter 30 keeps that many
samples from before the trigger. Status and diagnostics shows a whole capture.
    

      

    1. Set the signals (24, 25, 26), the divider (27), the pre-trigger (30) and, for 3 and 4, the level (29).
      2. 

    2. Write the trigger (28). Parameter 31 reads 1 (armed), 2 (recording), then 3 (done).
      3. 

    3. Read parameter 32 (samples readable) and 33 (the trigger sample's index, −1 if none).
      4. 

    4. Send_Buffer_Read with kind 0, first 0, count = parameter 32: one Respond_Buffer_Data frame per sample, oldest
   first.
      5. 

    

    The histogram works the same way with parameters 34 to 36 and kind 1: it adds a signal into 256 bins by the
electrical angle or by the encoder, every control loop, until it is stopped or one bin holds 65535 samples.
    Sound¶
    

    Sound through the motor: tones, a queue of timed events and samples, added to the output voltage while the
driver holds or moves. Sound explains how to use them; parameters 80 to 82 are in the
parameter table.
    

    Send_Sound¶
    

    

    ID 44host → driver8 bytesPython: Send_Sound()
    

    

    Plays sound through the motor: a tone now, a queue of timed events, or samples. The sound is added to the output voltage, on the q axis by default (parameter 82), and only while the driver holds or moves (position, velocity, current or PD mode); idle or an error stops all of it. Up to 4 tones sound at once (channels 0 to 3), from 20 Hz to below 2500 Hz, half the control-loop rate. Loudness is given as a current: the driver turns it into a voltage with the coil's impedance at that pitch, so low and high notes sound alike. The total voltage is limited to parameter 81 (2000 mV by default; 0 refuses sound). Added in firmware 109. The driver does not answer; parameter 80 says what plays. The Python library wraps the operations as Send_Tone, Send_Sound_Stop, Send_Sound_Event, Send_Sound_Play, Send_Sound_Clear, Send_Samples_Play and Send_Samples_Stop.
    

    8 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0
Operation
unsigned 8-bit
0 stop everything (tones, queue, samples); 1 a tone now; 2 add an event to the queue; 3 play the queue; 4 play the samples; 5 samples off, buffer cleared; 6 clear the queue (tones that sound keep sounding)

    

    
1
Channel
unsigned 8-bit
the tone channel, 0-3 (operations 1 and 2)

    

    
2-3
A
unsigned 16-bit
1, 2: the pitch in Hz (2: 0 silences the channel); 4: the sample rate in Hz, at most the loop rate

    

    
4-5
B
unsigned 16-bit
1, 2: the loudness in mA; 4: the voltage of a full-scale sample in mV

    

    
6-7
C
unsigned 16-bit
1: the duration in ms, 0 = until stopped; 2: the wait in ms before the next event, 0 = the next one starts with it

    
    

    

    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    The queue holds 31 events and is played by the control loop with loop-exact timing: an event sets one channel's pitch and loudness, then waits. A chord is three events with wait 0 and a fourth with the chord's length. When the queue runs out, every channel falls silent. An event sent to a full queue is dropped. Samples: send them first with Send_Sound_Samples, then operation 4; the driver plays them at the given rate and stops when the buffer runs dry. A frame of another length, or a remote frame, is ignored. It does not reset the watchdog.
    

    

    Send_Sound_Samples¶
    

    

    ID 45host → driver1 to 8 bytesPython: Send_Samples()
    

    

    Appends 1 to 8 samples (signed 8-bit, one per byte, −127 to 127 = full scale) to the driver's 1024-place sample buffer, which holds 1023 samples. Samples that do not fit are dropped; parameter 80 says how many are buffered. Send_Sound with operation 4 plays them. Added in firmware 109. The driver does not answer.
    

    8 bytes — eight samples; a shorter frame carries fewer
    

    

    


    
Byte
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0
Sample 1
signed 8-bit
the next sample

    

    
1
Sample 2
signed 8-bit


    

    
2
Sample 3
signed 8-bit


    

    
3
Sample 4
signed 8-bit


    

    
4
Sample 5
signed 8-bit


    

    
5
Sample 6
signed 8-bit


    

    
6
Sample 7
signed 8-bit


    

    
7
Sample 8
signed 8-bit


    
    

    

    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    Gripper¶
    

    The commands of gripper mode (#Gripper 1, saved).
    

    Send_gripper_data_pack¶
    

    

    ID 61host → driver0 or 5 bytesPython: Send_gripper_data_pack()
    

    

    The gripper command, for a driver set up as a gripper (#Gripper 1, saved). Position, speed and the current limit of the move, plus the activate, action and E-stop bits. A move is carried out only while the gripper is calibrated, activated and the action bit is 1. An empty frame (0 bytes) only asks for the state.
    

    5 bytes
    

    

    


    
Byte
Bit
Field
Type
Meaning

    
    


    
0

Position
unsigned 8-bit
0 fully open, 255 fully closed

    

    
1

Speed
unsigned 8-bit
0 slowest, 255 fastest

    

    
2-3

Current
signed 16-bit
current limit of the move in mA; from firmware 109 its magnitude is used and it stops at 2800 mA; see the gripper manual for the safe range; from firmware 110 the gripper's own limit, the driver's current limit (parameter 16) stays as set

    

    
4
7
Activate
bit
1 activate the gripper, 0 deactivate

    

    
4
6
Action
bit
1 go to the position, 0 idle

    

    
4
5
E-stop
bit
1 raises the gripper E-stop error

    

    
4
4
Release direction
bit
read by the firmware but not used

    
    

    

    

    The driver answers with Respond_Gripper_data_pack.
    

    

    Send_gripper_calibrate¶
    

    

    ID 62host → driver0 bytesPython: Send_gripper_calibrate()
    

    

    Starts the gripper calibration (the jaws find their end stops). Needed after every power-up. From firmware 110 each end is read under a light push and positions 0 and 255 lie a few ticks inside the two ends; this frame sent during a calibration starts it again from its first step. Parameter 93 gives the stroke found.
    

    The driver does not answer this command.
    

    

    Respond_Gripper_data_pack¶
    

    

    ID 60driver → host4 bytes
    

    

    The driver's answer to Send_gripper_data_pack: position, measured current, and the state bits. Python attributes gripper_position, gripper_current, gripper_activated, gripper_action_status, gripper_object_detection, gripper_temperature_error, gripper_timeout_error, gripper_estop_error, gripper_calibrated. The Python library 1.20 and older combine the two object-detection bits the wrong way round, so their gripper_object_detection reads 2 for an object found while closing and 1 for one found while opening; 1.21 and newer read them as the table says.
    

    The frame (4 bytes)

the action bit of the last Send_gripper_data_pack (1 after #Grippos); the driver never clears it, so it stays 1 after the move ends: the object detection tells when the move has ended

    UART commands
                        
    
                    
    UART commands¶
    

    Every command the firmware release this site describes understands over the serial port. The kind, the argument
type, the mode a command enters and what it prints are read from the firmware; the descriptions are written by
hand. Over UART explains the protocol.
    

    Syntax¶
    

    #<Name> [number]

    

      

    • A Set/Get command sets the value when a number follows and prints the current value either way.
      • 

    • A Get command only prints.
      • 

    • An Action command does something; a number after it is ignored.
      • 

    • Names are case-sensitive, at most 19 characters; the argument is one word of at most 19 characters, without
  spaces, so a list is written with commas (#WdFb 2000,0,1000,2, #Par 1,200); firmware 107 and older allowed 20
  characters for name and number together. The line ends with LF (CR before it is
  fine). 256000 baud, 8N1, 3.3 V.
      • 

    • A name the firmware does not know is answered Unknown command.
      •

    By group¶
    

      

    • Motion: setpoints and modes, idle and the E-stop (Estop, from firmware 110)
      • 

    • Gains and limits: the loop gains, the limits, the integrator reset, the current loop bandwidth
      • 

    • Calibration and motor parameters: Cal and Calonly, the motor's R, L and pole pairs, the constants calibration finds, the calibration settings, the encoder table (Encal) and the anti-cogging (Cogcal)
      • 

    • Status and utility: save, reset, defaults, identity, errors, temperature, the cyclic output, any parameter (Par), the tool id, the watchdog fallback, the product lock
      • 

    • Gripper: gripper mode
      • 

    Motion¶
    

    

    #Iq #Id #V #P #PD #PDV #PDI #Uq #Ud #Openloop #Idle #Estop #Brake #Close
    

    

    #Iq¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: current setpoint in mAEnters current mode
    

    

    Current (torque) control with the given Iq setpoint.
    

    Response: the measured Iq in mA
    

    

    #Id¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: current setpoint in mA
    

    

    Sets the Id setpoint; normally left at 0. Also parameter 58.
    

    Response: the measured Id in mA
    

    

    #V¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: speed setpoint in encoder ticks/sEnters velocity mode
    

    

    Velocity control with the given speed.
    

    Response: the measured speed in ticks/s
    

    

    #P¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: position setpoint in encoder ticksEnters position mode
    

    

    Position control to the given position.
    

    Response: the measured position in ticks
    

    

    #PD¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: position setpoint in encoder ticksEnters PD mode
    

    

    Impedance (PD) control around the given position with the gains KP and KD.
    

    Response: the measured position in ticks
    

    

    #PDV¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: speed setpoint in encoder ticks/s
    

    

    The speed setpoint of the PD loop.
    

    Response: the PD speed setpoint
    

    

    #PDI¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: current feedforward in mA
    

    

    The current feedforward of the PD loop.
    

    Response: the PD current feedforward
    

    

    #Uq¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: voltage in mVEnters voltage torque mode
    

    

    Voltage-torque mode with the given q-axis voltage (no current loop).
    

    Response: the Uq setpoint in mV
    

    

    #Ud¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: voltage in mV
    

    

    The d-axis voltage used in voltage-torque mode; does not change the mode.
    

    Response: the Ud setpoint in mV
    

    

    #Openloop¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: electrical speed in rad/sEnters open loop mode
    

    

    Turns the stator field at the given electrical speed without the encoder in the loop, with the voltage set by Openv. Without a number it still starts the motor, at the stored speed: #Openloop alone is not a query. The speed is the same setting as #Opens.
    

    Response: the open-loop speed
    

    

    #Idle¶
    

    

    ActionEnters idle mode
    

    

    Goes to idle; the motor coasts or brakes as set by Brake.
    

    Response: Idle
    

    

    #Estop¶
    

    

    Action
    

    

    The E-stop, as Send_ESTOP over CAN: it raises the E-stop error, the driver disables the motor, which coasts, and a calibration, gripper calibration or encoder-table or anti-cogging run that is running ends. The error stays until Clear. Added in firmware 110.
    

    Response: E-stop
    

    

    #Brake¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 or 1
    

    

    What the motor does in idle: 1 brakes (the phases are shorted through the driver), 0 coasts (the phases are open, the default). Takes effect once the driver is idle, so send Idle first. From firmware 110 Save keeps it; on 109 the driver coasted again after a power cycle. Also parameter 18. An error always disables the motor, which then coasts. The braking current is not limited, see Control modes. Added in firmware 107.
    

    Response: the setting
    

    

    #Close¶
    

    

    ActionEnters idle mode
    

    

    Leaves open loop and goes back to closed-loop idle. From firmware 110, while a calibration (Cal, Calonly) runs, it stops the calibration instead: it ends as a failed one (Calibration failed!), with the motor released and the driver not calibrated until it calibrates again. Firmware 109 switched the mode under the running calibration.
    

    Response: nothing; Close: the calibration is stopped during a calibration (firmware 110)
    

    Gains and limits¶
    

    

    #Kpp #Kpv #Kiv #Kpiq #Kiiq #Kpid #Kiid #KP #KD #Vlimit #Ilim #Vlim #Rstint #Cbw
    

    

    #Kpp¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: gain
    

    

    Kp of the position loop.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Kpv¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: gain
    

    

    Kp of the velocity loop.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Kiv¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: gain
    

    

    Ki of the velocity loop.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Kpiq¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: gain
    

    

    Kp of the Iq current loop; also sets Kpid.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Kiiq¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: gain
    

    

    Ki of the Iq current loop; also sets Kiid.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Kpid¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: gain
    

    

    Kp of the Id current loop.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Kiid¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: gain
    

    

    Ki of the Id current loop.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #KP¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: gain
    

    

    KP of the impedance (PD) loop.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #KD¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: gain
    

    

    KD of the impedance (PD) loop.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Vlimit¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: speed in encoder ticks/s
    

    

    The speed limit: the speed the position loop may command, and the largest velocity setpoint. (The velocity integrator is clamped to the current limit.) The magnitude of a negative number is taken.
    

    Response: the current limit
    

    

    #Ilim¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: current in mA
    

    

    The Iq current limit. Set it to the rated phase current of your motor; the firmware clamps it to 2800 mA and takes the magnitude of a negative number.
    

    Response: the current limit
    

    

    #Vlim¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: voltage in mV
    

    

    The voltage limit of the current loops (Ud and Uq); 0 means use the supply voltage.
    

    Response: the current limit in mV
    

    

    #Rstint¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 or 1
    

    

    Whether the integral accumulators are reset on every new setpoint. From firmware 110 also parameter 90.
    

    Response: the setting
    

    

    #Cbw¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: bandwidth in Hz, 10 to 500
    

    

    The current loop bandwidth. From firmware 109 setting it computes Kpiq and Kiiq at once from R and L, the way calibration does, and prints them (the same as parameter 49); a value outside 10-500, or with R or L at 0, changes nothing. Not saved: 160 Hz after a power cycle, but the gains it set are kept by Save.
    

    Response: the bandwidth, then Kp_iq = …, Ki_iq = …
    

    Calibration and motor parameters¶
    

    

    #Cal #Calonly #Encal #Cogcal #R #L #PP #Kt #KV #Flux #Offset #Iqoffset #Magnet #Dir #Phase #Resv #Calpwr #Openv #Opens #Vp #Calibrated
    

    

    #Cal¶
    

    

    ActionEnters idle mode
    

    

    Runs the calibration routine (see Calibration): it measures R and L, finds the pole pairs, the phase order and the angle offset, and measures Kt, KV and the flux linkage. It uses its own 1600 mA current limit for its steps; from firmware 109 it puts the limit that was in force back when it ends. From firmware 109, when it succeeds, the encoder table and then the anti-cogging follow by themselves, and everything is saved (about a minute; keep the shaft free).
    

    Response: the calibration log
    

    

    #Calonly¶
    

    

    ActionEnters idle mode
    

    

    The calibration alone: like Cal, but nothing follows it. Save afterwards with Save. Added in firmware 109.
    

    Response: the calibration log
    

    

    #Encal¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument nothing, or save, all, stop, on, off, state
    

    

    The encoder table (see Encoder table and anti-cogging). Alone it measures the table and uses it: the field turns the rotor open loop one shaft turn each way at 2048 ticks/s, about 20 s, with the shaft free. save also saves; all then identifies the anti-cogging and saves both; stop aborts; on and off switch the correction (on only with a measured table, and not while a run turns the motor); state or any other word only prints. The same as parameters 52 and 53. Added in firmware 109; not yet tested on a Spectral Micro.
    

    Response: Encoder table: <state>, then the table's error in degrees
    

    

    #Cogcal¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument nothing, or save, trim, stop, state
    

    

    The anti-cogging identification on the drive (see Encoder table and anti-cogging): one motor turn forward and one back in velocity mode at parameter 44's speed (1500 ticks/s, about 22 s), the shaft free to turn. save also saves; stop aborts, also the encoder part of a combined run; state or any other word only prints. trim identifies like the plain command on the Spectral Micro: its firmware skips the current-sense trim. Added in firmware 109; not yet tested on a Spectral Micro.
    

    Response: Anti-cogging identification: <state>, the speed and the identified cogging in mA rms, and the harmonics when done
    

    

    #R¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: resistance in Ω
    

    

    The phase resistance of the motor, measured by Cal; it can also be set by hand. Also parameter 76, in mΩ.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #L¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: inductance in H
    

    

    The phase inductance of the motor, measured by Cal; it can also be set by hand. Also parameter 77, in µH.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #PP¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: pole pairs
    

    

    The motor's pole pairs, found by Cal; they can also be set by hand. Also parameter 78: 1-49, only while idle (50 and more would read as a STEPFOC calibration); from 109 #PP answers PP: not taken (1-49, and only while idle) otherwise.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Kt¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: Nm/A
    

    

    The torque constant, found by calibration.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #KV¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: rpm/V
    

    

    The velocity constant, found by calibration.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Flux¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: Wb
    

    

    The flux linkage, found by calibration. Works over UART from firmware 109; the parser of 107 and older accepted only the lowercase #flux, which had no handler.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Offset¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: angle in rad
    

    

    The angle offset between encoder zero and electrical zero, found by calibration.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Iqoffset¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: current in mA
    

    

    The current used by the angle-offset search and the commutation check of Cal, 300 mA by default. Not saved.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Magnet¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Reads the encoder's magnet status.
    

    Response: Magnet Good or Magnet Bad
    

    

    #Dir¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 or 1
    

    

    The base direction of the encoder and phase mapping. Calibration always sets it to 1 (0 after #Default); the direction it finds is the commutation direction, which #Info prints.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Phase¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Prints the phase order found by calibration.
    

    Response: Phase order is: N
    

    

    #Resv¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: voltage in mV
    

    

    The voltage the resistance test of Cal starts at; it rises until the power reaches Calpwr. Not saved.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Calpwr¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: power in W
    

    

    The power the resistance and inductance tests of Cal may dissipate in the motor. Not saved.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Openv¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: voltage in mV
    

    

    The voltage of the open-loop spin, in calibration and with Openloop. Not saved.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Opens¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument float: electrical speed in rad/s
    

    

    The speed of the open-loop spin in calibration; the same setting as the speed of #Openloop.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Vp¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: voltage in mV
    

    

    The voltage of the phase-order search in calibration. Not saved.
    

    Response: the current value
    

    

    #Calibrated¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 or 1
    

    

    The calibrated flag. #Calibrated 1 clears a calibration error without calibrating; the motor may still be uncalibrated.
    

    Response: the flag
    

    Status and utility¶
    

    

    #Save #Reset #Default #Pullconfig #Info #Tone #Par #Tool #WdFb #Product #Param #Help #Error #Clear #Mode #Vbus #Vmax #Temp #Term #Tlim #Iabc #PWM #LED #CANID #SERNUM #Activate #Cyc #Cyca
    

    

    #Save¶
    

    

    Action
    

    

    Writes the configuration to the EEPROM. Do it after calibration and after tuning.
    

    Response: Save
    

    

    #Reset¶
    

    

    Action
    

    

    Resets the driver. From firmware 104 the reset lands in the bootloader, which waits 2 s for a flashing host and then starts the firmware again.
    

    Response: nothing
    

    

    #Default¶
    

    

    Action
    

    

    Writes the factory defaults to the EEPROM and loads them: the calibration is erased (Not calibrated), the gripper flag, heartbeat and watchdog are cleared, the hardware version and batch date are set to 1 and 24092024, and 1111 is stored as the serial number; from firmware 109 also the tool id (0), the watchdog fallback, and the anti-cogging set, the encoder table, its trims and parameter 56 (cleared). From firmware 109 the CAN node ID is kept (0 only when no valid one is stored). Note the values first.
    

    Response: Loaded default config!
    

    

    #Pullconfig¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Prints the whole configuration on one line, for Motor GUI.
    

    Response: the configuration
    

    

    #Info¶
    

    

    Action
    

    

    Prints the driver's identity and the motor: hardware version, batch date, firmware version, ISR time, CAN ID, calibrated or not, temperature, Vbus, Dir, phase order, commutation direction, the speeds of the calibration spin, pole pairs, R, L, Kt and KV. From firmware 109 also the build (Build:, the git commit, with + when built with uncommitted changes), the Board ID (this board, from the chip's unique number; not the bootloader's board ID), the Calibration ID (new at every successful calibration), the CAN receive overruns and dropped transmits, the product, the current-sense zeros, the tool id, the watchdog and its fallback, settings block 2, the anti-cogging, and at the end the In force: section (see Status and diagnostics). The serial number is #SERNUM, the gains #Param, the errors #Error.
    

    Response: the report
    

    

    #Tone¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument f,mA[,ms], or offEnters position mode
    

    

    A tone through the motor (see Sound): #Tone 440,250,1000 plays 440 Hz at a loudness of 250 mA for 1000 ms, on the axis parameter 82 picks. mA left out: 200; ms left out: 500; ms 0: until #Tone off. 20 Hz up to below 2500 Hz. It plays only while the driver holds or moves; from idle a calibrated driver first holds the position it is at (#Idle releases it). #Tone alone or #Tone off stops all sound. Added in firmware 109.
    

    Response: Tone 440 Hz, 250 mA, 1000 ms; from idle first Holding the position while it plays (#Idle releases it); Tone: not played (…) when it cannot; Tone off
    

    

    #Par¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: n, or n,value
    

    

    Any parameter by its number (see Parameters and buffers): alone it lists every parameter, #Par 1 reads one, #Par 1,200 sets it (no spaces); a gain or limit (8-16, 20) takes a decimal, e.g. #Par 9,0.02 sets Kpv. Added in firmware 109.
    

    Response: P<n> = <value>; refused: out of range, read only, not now, or unknown when the driver did not take it; P<n> unknown
    

    

    #Tool¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 to 255
    

    

    The tool id: what the drive is built into, 0 none, 1 SSG-48, 11-33 MSG (tens = rail 1/2/3 = 100/150/200 mm, units = stepper 1/2/3 = 21.5/40/60 mm), 200-254 your own. The same as parameter 1. Kept by Save. Added in firmware 109.
    

    Response: Tool ID: N, or Invalid tool id (0-255)
    

    

    #WdFb¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int list: time_ms,current_mA,speed_ticks_s,after_action
    

    

    The watchdog fallback, four numbers at once without spaces, e.g. #WdFb 2000,0,1000,2: how long hold or descend runs, descend's current and speed cap, and what follows (0 legacy, 2 brake, 3 coast). The same as parameters 2 to 5, clamped the same way; fewer than four numbers change nothing. Kept by Save. Added in firmware 109.
    

    Response: Watchdog fallback: … ms, … mA, … ticks/s, then …
    

    

    #Product¶
    

    

    ActionArgument nothing, or claim, clear
    

    

    The product lock: prints this firmware and the board's stamp. claim stamps the board as this product (a repaired board, a motor the stamp rule misjudges), clear removes the stamp; power-cycle after either. Over UART only. Added in firmware 109.
    

    Response: Firmware: Spectral Micro; board stamp: …
    

    

    #Param¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Prints every gain of every loop.
    

    Response: the gains
    

    

    #Help¶
    

    

    Action
    

    

    Prints a short command reference. Added in firmware 106.
    

    Response: the reference
    

    

    #Error¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Prints which errors are active.
    

    Response: No errors! or In error state!, then one line per active error (see Errors and LED states), and FAULT pin is LOW: the gate driver reports a fault while the driver stage's fault pin is active
    

    

    #Clear¶
    

    

    ActionEnters idle mode
    

    

    Clears the errors and goes to idle. An error whose cause is still present comes straight back; a calibration error is cleared only by calibrating (or #Calibrated 1).
    

    Response: Clear errors
    

    

    #Mode¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Prints the control mode. In voltage-torque mode (#Uq) it prints nothing.
    

    Response: Idle mode, Position control, Speed control, Current control, PD control, Open loop, Gripper mode, Gripper calib mode
    

    

    #Vbus¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Prints the supply voltage.
    

    Response: mV
    

    

    #Vmax¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: voltage in mV
    

    

    The supply voltage above which a Vbus error is raised: 29000 mV after #Default.
    

    Response: Vmax in mV: N
    

    

    #Temp¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Prints the thermistor temperature.
    

    Response: °C, or Temp Off while the thermistor is disabled
    

    

    #Term¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 or 1
    

    

    Enables the thermistor measurement and the temperature error (off by default). From firmware 110 also parameter 91.
    

    Response: the setting
    

    

    #Tlim¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: temperature in °C
    

    

    The temperature at which the temperature error is raised; 75 by default.
    

    Response: Temp limit N
    

    

    #Iabc¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Prints the phase currents.
    

    Response: Ia, Ib, Ic in mA
    

    

    #PWM¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Prints the PWM duty values.
    

    Response: the duties
    

    

    #LED¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 or 1
    

    

    Turns the status LED on or off.
    

    Response: the LED state
    

    

    #CANID¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 to 13
    

    

    The CAN node ID. Use 0 to 13: the firmware accepts 14 and 15, but the bootloader reserves them and a driver on one of those cannot be updated over CAN. Kept over a power cycle after Save, which also copies it into the bootloader's board ID; from firmware 109 Default and a settings reset keep it too.
    

    Response: the ID, or Invalid CAN ID
    

    

    #SERNUM¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: number
    

    

    The serial number. Save writes it to the EEPROM, but the firmware does not read it back at power-up: after a power cycle the driver reports 1.
    

    Response: Serial number is: N
    

    

    #Activate¶
    

    

    Action
    

    

    Sets the controller's activated flag (bit 6 of byte 1 of Send_Respond_State_of_Errors). It is not the gripper's activate bit, which #Gripact and Send_gripper_data_pack set.
    

    Response: Controller activated
    

    

    #Cyc¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: interval in ms (0 = off)
    

    

    Sends position, speed and Iq on its own every interval: $ <position> <speed> <Iq>; for BetterSerialPlotter.
    

    Response: Cyclic disabled for #Cyc without a number; #Cyc 0 stops the output without an answer
    

    

    #Cyca¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: interval in ms (0 = off)
    

    

    Like Cyc, in the Arduino plotter format Position:x,Speed:y,Iq:z.
    

    Response: Cyclic disabled for #Cyca without a number; #Cyca 0 stops the output without an answer
    

    Gripper¶
    

    

    #Gripper #Gripcal #Grippos #Gripvel #Gripcur #Gripact #Gripstop #Gripinfo
    

    

    #Gripper¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 or 1
    

    

    Whether this driver is a gripper. Save it; #Default clears it. From firmware 110 also parameter 92, which takes a write only while the driver is idle.
    

    Response: the flag
    

    

    #Gripcal¶
    

    

    ActionEnters gripper calibration mode
    

    

    Starts the gripper calibration (the jaws find their end stops). From firmware 110 each end is read under a light 100 mA push after the 500 mA one, and 0 and 255 lie a few ticks inside the two ends; the gripper stays uncalibrated when the ends lie less than 1000 ticks apart, and Gripcal during a calibration starts it again from its first step. Parameter 93 then gives the stroke in ticks.
    

    Response: Gripper calibration!
    

    

    #Grippos¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 (open) to 255 (closed)Enters gripper mode
    

    

    The position setpoint; also activates the gripper, sets the action bit and goes to gripper mode.
    

    Response: the current position
    

    

    #Gripvel¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 to 255
    

    

    The speed setpoint of gripper moves.
    

    Response: the setpoint
    

    

    #Gripcur¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: current in mA
    

    

    The current limit of gripper moves. From firmware 109 a gripper move uses its magnitude and stops at 2800 mA; see the gripper manual for the safe range. From firmware 110 it is the gripper's own limit: the driver's current limit (Ilim) stays as set.
    

    Response: the setpoint
    

    

    #Gripact¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 or 1
    

    

    The activate bit.
    

    Response: the bit
    

    

    #Gripstop¶
    

    

    Set/GetArgument int: 0 or 1
    

    

    The gripper E-stop bit.
    

    Response: the bit
    

    

    #Gripinfo¶
    

    

    Get
    

    

    Prints the gripper state: whether the driver is a gripper, calibrated, activated, the position, speed and current setpoints, the E-stop, action and object-detection status, and the end stops.
    

    Response: the report
    

    Defaults¶
    

    The settings of the firmware, read from its source at the release this site describes. The table leaves out
the firmware's internal state (counters, flags, scratch variables).
    

      

    • Start value is the value in the firmware's structures (common.h). At power-up the firmware loads the saved
  settings from the EEPROM over it, so for a setting that #Save keeps the start value only shows until the EEPROM
  is read.
      • 

    • After #Default is what the driver runs with after #Default, which writes the factory values to the EEPROM
  and loads them. A fresh board, and a board whose saved configuration fails its check at power-up, gets the same
  values.
      • 

    • Kept by #Save says whether the setting survives a power cycle. No means the driver starts with the start
  value again after every power-up.
      • 

    

    #Default also erases the calibration; from firmware 109 it keeps a valid stored CAN node ID (existing_id in
the table; 0 when none is stored); see Saving the configuration. A name after a value,
such as 160 (CURRENT_BANDWIDTH_DEFAULT), is the constant in constants.h the value comes from. The settings of
firmware 109 that are parameters, the anti-cogging and the encoder table among them, are in the
parameter table with their defaults.

    Errors and LED states¶
    

    The LED¶
    

    


    
Pattern
State

    
    


    
three short flashes, a pause
normal operation: calibrated, no error

    

    
fast flashing, about 10 times a second
calibration running

    

    
blinking for about 2 s after power-up
the bootloader waits for a host before it starts the firmware

    

    
on, steady
error mode, or not calibrated

    

    
off
no power, or #LED 0

    
    

    

    


    
Normal operation
Calibration
Error mode

    
    


    




    
    

    

    The error flags¶
    

    The flags as the firmware keeps them, the line #Error prints for each, and where each sits in the two bytes of
Send_Respond_State_of_Errors (bits numbered from the most significant).
    

    


    
Flag
#Error prints
Byte, bit
Raised when

    
    


    
Error
In error state!
0, 7
any of the flags below is set (also the error bit of every CAN identifier)

    

    
temperature_error
Temp error
0, 6
the thermistor reads above #Tlim or below −20 °C (with #Term 1)

    

    
encoder_error
Encoder error
0, 5
the magnet is not detected or the reading fails its check

    

    
Vbus_error
Vbus error
0, 4
the supply is below 9 V or above #Vmax (29 V after #Default); from firmware 109 a low supply is not checked in the first 500 ms after power-up

    

    
Driver_error
Drv error
0, 3
the MOSFET driver reported a fault on its fault pin: undervoltage, thermal shutdown or overcurrent. #Error also prints FAULT pin is LOW: the gate driver reports a fault while the fault output is still active

    

    
Velocity_error
Velocity error
0, 2
the measured speed exceeds 20,000,000 ticks/s (about 1220 revolutions per second), a fixed limit: a runaway or an encoder fault, not #Vlimit

    

    
Current_error
Current error
0, 1
never in this firmware; reserved

    

    
ESTOP_error
Received ESTOP command
0, 0
Send_ESTOP was received, or from firmware 110 #Estop over UART

    

    
Calibrated (0)
Not calibrated
1, 7 (1 = calibrated)
the motor has not been calibrated

    

    
Activated
—
1, 6
the activated flag, not an error

    

    
Watchdog_error
Watchdog error
1, 5
the watchdog time passed without a motion command or an answered request over CAN

    

    
wrong_product
Wrong firmware for this board (product lock)
1, 4
from firmware 109: the firmware found the other product's stamp, or a calibration its product cannot make, at start-up; it drives nothing

    
    

    

    Gripper mode has its own flags in Respond_Gripper_data_pack: a temperature error (the driver's temperature
error), a timeout error (the driver's watchdog error: the CAN watchdog ran out) and an E-stop error; see the
Gripper reference.
    

    Clearing¶
    

    #Clear and Send_Clear_Error clear every flag except Calibrated and go to idle; a flag whose cause persists is
set again at once. While any flag is set the driver disables the motor, which coasts whatever #Brake says. The watchdog, E-stop and velocity errors clear and stay clear; the Vbus, encoder, driver and
temperature errors come back until the cause is gone. Calibration clears the calibration error. A wrong product
stays until the right firmware is flashed. Clearing a watchdog error also ends a watchdog hold or descend.
    Python library¶
    

    The Spectral-BLDC package (Spectral-BLDC-Python,
pip install Spectral-BLDC) has one method per CAN message in Spectral_BLDC.SpectralCAN, and
Spectral_BLDC.CanCommunication for the bus. With Python shows how they are used.
